<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Virasoro conjecture for the ordinary descendant Gromov–Witten theory of smooth complete intersections in projective space, in every genus and curve class and with arbitrary cohomology insertions. This includes primitive and odd cohomology classes, with no semisimplicity assumption.

- **claim-main-virasoro** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X\subset\mathbb P^N_\mathbb C$ be a smooth complete intersection. Its ordinary, unreduced total descendant potential satisfies the Virasoro constraints in every genus and curve class, with arbitrary insertions from $H^*(X;\mathbb C)$.
  - Explanation: The Virasoro constraints hold for the ordinary unreduced descendant potential of every smooth complete intersection in projective space, in all genera and curve classes, with arbitrary complex cohomology insertions. Primitive and odd classes are included and no semisimplicity is assumed.
- **claim-positive-coefficient-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-positive-rule)): A labelled coefficient of $L_k^XZ_X$, for fixed $k>0$, is a finite linear combination of the following operations on the disconnected Gromov–Witten tensors:* 1. *add one special unit marking, with prescribed descendant and $\mathcal R_X$-powers;* 2. *act on one existing marking, changing its descendant exponent and applying an $\mathcal R_X$-power, with a coefficient polynomial in its first Hodge degree;* 3. *add two markings joined by the coevaluation of $(\, ,\,)_X$, with descendant powers, first-Hodge-degree polynomials, and $\mathcal R_X$-powers on its legs;* 4. *remove two primary markings, leaving their two cohomology inputs in the classical pairing with $\mathcal R_X^{k+1}$, multiplied by the remaining disconnected Gromov–Witten tensor.* *The numerical coefficients and genus powers are universal for targets of fixed dimension. At most two existing labels are used nonordinarily in one summand.
  - Explanation: For fixed positive k, each labelled coefficient of the operator applied to the potential is a finite combination of four explicit operations on disconnected Gromov-Witten tensors. At most two existing labels are used nonordinarily in a summand.
- **claim-first-page-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-pairing)): The complex $C_Y$ has a perfect graded-symmetric chain pairing of degree $2n$ and Tate type $\mathbb Q(-n)$. On elements of total degrees $k$ and $2n-k$ it is $$\begin{aligned} \label{fp:pairing-formula} B_Y\bigl((v,a,b),(w,a',b')\bigr) ={}&\sum_v\int_{Y_v}v_v\cup w_v +(-1)^k\sum_e\int_{D_e}a_e\cup b'_e\\ &+(-1)^{k+1}\sum_e\int_{D_e}b_e\cup a'_e.\notag \end{aligned}$$ It induces the weight-graded nearby Poincaré pairing under <a href="#fp:abutment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fp:abutment">[fp:abutment]</a>. Its categorical inverse-pairing tensor $\Delta_{C_Y}$ is closed and is a sum of tensors supported on a single vertex or a single edge.
  - Explanation: The total complex of the weight spectral sequence carries a perfect graded-symmetric chain pairing. Its inverse tensor is closed and splits into vertex-supported and edge-supported pieces.
- **claim-first-hodge-index-chain-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-first-index)): On $C_Y\otimes\mathbb C$, let $P$ act by the first Hodge index, including the Tate twist. Then $Pd=dP$. Consequently $(f(P)\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{C_Y}$ is closed for every function $f$ on the finite spectrum of $P$.
  - Explanation: The first Hodge index operator commutes with the differential of the complex, so functions of it applied to the inverse pairing tensor stay closed.
- **claim-ordered-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-ordered-comparison)): The residue formula for $\phi_Z$, restricted to increasing tuples, gives <a href="#fp:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fp:comparison">[fp:comparison]</a> with the properties stated above.
  - Explanation: Restricting the residue comparison map to increasing tuples gives a bifiltered chain map into the ordered model that induces the limiting mixed Hodge isomorphism and an isomorphism of second weight pages.
- **claim-ordered-pullbacks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-pullback)): Each projection of the ordered configuration model to $\mathcal Y$ induces a bifiltered chain pullback of the $K$ models. On weight pages its terms are ordinary pullbacks on the relevant intersections and the maps on logarithmic generators modulo ordinary forms. These terms are determined by the incident strata, their normal data, and the chosen ordering.
  - Explanation: Each projection of the ordered configuration model induces a bifiltered chain pullback of the ordered models, whose weight-page terms are ordinary pullbacks determined by incident strata.
- **claim-trace-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-trace-normalization)): Normalize the residue and Tate factors so that the trace of an extending top class is the sum of its component integrals. Under multiplicative nearby comparison, the descended trace is ordinary integration on every connected nearby component. The first-page pairing of <a href="#fp:pairing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fp:pairing">4</a> consequently induces nearby Poincaré duality with this normalization.
  - Explanation: With the stated normalization the descended trace is ordinary integration on nearby components, so the first-page pairing induces nearby Poincare duality.
- **claim-leading-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-leading-class)): Suppose $\widetilde{\mathcal Y^{\,r}}\to\Delta$ is smooth and semistable and carries an algebraic class $\Gamma$ of codimension $c$ restricting to a prescribed correspondence on the smooth fiber. The induced weight-graded specialization of this correspondence is represented on the first page by the ordinary restrictions $\Gamma\|_{Z_v}$ to the smooth components of its central fiber, inserted through the specialization morphism into $K_Z$. The same assertion holds with fixed smooth projective source factors and algebraic correspondences extending over the total family, by first exposing their cohomology slots and then applying fixed homogeneous inputs.
  - Explanation: The weight-graded specialization of an extending algebraic correspondence is represented on the first page by its ordinary restrictions to the components of the central fiber.
- **claim-scalar-diagram-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-scalar-limit)): Consider a closed tensor diagram made from flat Hodge tensors of prescribed types, flat singles of fixed Hodge type, pairings and inverse pairings, and functions of the first Hodge index on its legs. Assume its total type is that of a scalar. Its value on the smooth fibers is locally constant and equals the value obtained on the associated Hodge and weight gradeds of the limiting mixed Hodge structures, with all Tate shifts retained.
  - Explanation: A closed scalar tensor diagram built from flat Hodge data and first-index functions is locally constant on smooth fibers and equals its value on the Hodge and weight gradeds of the limit.
- **claim-chain-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-chain-contraction)): Let $C$ be a finite complex with a perfect chain pairing and let $T$ be a chain functional on a tensor product of copies of $C$, possibly with fixed cycles and fixed degree shifts. Contract any paired slots against its closed inverse-pairing tensor. The resulting scalar is the contraction of the induced functional on cohomology against the induced inverse pairing. The assertion is unchanged if a pairing leg is acted on by a chain map.
  - Explanation: Contracting a chain functional against the closed inverse-pairing tensor of a perfect finite complex equals the contraction of the induced cohomological functional against the induced inverse pairing.
- **claim-local-first-page-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-local-algebra)): Fix the arities of a scalar test as in <a href="#sec:conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conventions">2</a>, applied to one coefficient operation of <a href="#conv:positive-rule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:positive-rule">2</a>. For each of its evaluation correspondences, assume the extending class and assignment-sensitive component restriction rule of <a href="#ev:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:restriction">16</a>. Assume its degree tests extend coherently and its singles are coherent admissible singles: extending algebraic classes and their complex linear combinations, or homogeneous cohomology inputs from fixed smooth projective sources through algebraic correspondences extending over the relevant total families and inducing flat Hodge maps on their smooth loci. Then its numerical value has an expansion with these properties.* 1. *Each term of its cohomological calculation uses only a bounded number of positions of the dual graph and closed incidence neighborhoods of bounded radius. The bounds depend on the fixed arities and dimensions, not on the length of inserted chains.* 2. *The terms are obtained from component restrictions of the evaluation classes, pullbacks, cups, residues, integrations, and the vertex and edge blocks of $\Delta_{C_Y}$. A Hodge-graded coevaluation uses one vertex or one edge neighborhood.* 3. *Identical component and stratum diagrams, normal data, orders, and restriction classes in these neighborhoods give identical terms, with the same signs and Tate factors. Changing the smoothing or its connectedness away from these neighborhoods imposes no extra condition on a term.* *Ordinary singles already integrated into an evaluation class need not be counted as exposed positions. If an operator subsequently acts on one of those singles, that slot must instead be exposed and counted.
  - Explanation: Each scalar test applied to a coefficient operation expands into terms supported on bounded incidence neighborhoods of the dual graph, with bounds independent of chain length.
- **claim-configuration-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-configuration)): The family $\mathcal Q_I\to B$ is projective and semistable, with smooth total space. Its projections to the factors of $\mathcal Y$ are logarithmic maps. Let $I=I_0\sqcup I_1$ be an assignment of the recorded marks to the two sides. The corresponding central component is canonically $$\label{ev:component-product} Q_{I_0,I_1} =\mathcal Q_{I_0}(X_0,D)\times \mathcal Q_{I_1}(X_1,D).$$ When a side is disconnected, this formula can be restricted to any prescribed connected target component for each recorded mark. The aligned configuration space of all marks on a side is retained in this formula.
  - Explanation: The space of stable expanded configurations is projective and semistable with smooth total space, and its central components are products of relative configuration spaces according to the side assignment of marks.
- **claim-aligned-cut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-aligned-cut)): Fix bounded discrete data for labelled connected source components in $\mathcal M$, permitting their disjoint union, and choose one cut of the globally aligned target expansion. Let $\eta$ range over the resulting splittings with ordered labels on their $r$ paired relative roots of contact orders $c_1,\ldots,c_r$. The side source graphs may contain rootless vertices or be empty. Let $\mathcal M_{\eta,i}$ denote the relative map stack of the *whole* side source graph over one shared side expansion. On the normalized target cut, the virtual boundary class is the sum, over these labelled splittings, of the gluing pushforwards of $$\label{ev:aligned-cut-formula} \frac{\prod_{j=1}^r c_j}{r!} \Delta_{D^r}^{!} \bigl([\mathcal M_{\eta,0}]^{\mathrm{vir}} \times[\mathcal M_{\eta,1}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\bigr).$$ There is no additional factor for a source component or a connected component of $D$. For $r=0$, the product and diagonal are the identity. The formula is an equality of virtual classes before evaluation pushforward. The $r!$ forgets the ordered root labels; the finite quotient by permutations of identical source components is taken on the whole indexed sum, not again on each root-labelled side factor.
  - Explanation: On the normalized target cut the virtual boundary class is a sum over labelled splittings of weighted refined diagonal pullbacks of the side virtual classes, with no extra factor for source components or components of the seam.
- **claim-restriction-assignments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-restriction)): The class $\Gamma_I$ is an actual extending correspondence on the smooth total configuration family. Let $i_\epsilon:Q_\epsilon\hookrightarrow\mathcal Q_I$ be a central component, with the recorded side and connected-component assignment $\epsilon$. Its restriction $i_\epsilon^*\Gamma_I$ is given by the virtual degeneration formula restricted to splitting data with that assignment, retaining on each side the joint relative evaluation into the corresponding factor of <a href="#ev:component-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ev:component-product">[ev:component-product]</a>. This is an equality of correspondence classes on $Q_\epsilon$ before further cohomology insertions are applied.* *Concretely, in the scheme case let $\eta$ range over admissible splittings with that assignment, with $r$ labelled matching relative roots of orders $c_1,\ldots,c_r$. Let $\mathcal M_{\eta,0}$ and $\mathcal M_{\eta,1}$ be the relative moduli, and let $$G_\eta=\mathcal M_{\eta,0}\times_{D^r}\mathcal M_{\eta,1}.$$ The root matching is the refined diagonal pullback. With the usual labelled-root convention, the restriction is the cycle class of $$\label{ev:restriction-formula} \sum_{\eta\text{ of assignment }\epsilon} \frac{\prod_{j=1}^r c_j}{r!}\, (e_\eta)_* \left( \Theta_\eta\cap \Delta_{D^r}^{!} \bigl([\mathcal M_{\eta,0}]^{\mathrm{vir}} \times[\mathcal M_{\eta,1}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\bigr) \right).$$ Here $e_\eta:G_\eta\to Q_\epsilon$ retains the two aligned relative evaluations; it is allowed to have image on the boundary of $Q_\epsilon$. Source-component labeling or quotient conventions are held fixed throughout. Equivalently, summing over unlabelled root data replaces $r!$ by the usual root-permutation stabilizer. For $I=\varnothing$ the assertion is the ordinary special-fiber virtual degeneration formula.
  - Explanation: The pushed-forward virtual class is an actual correspondence on the smooth total configuration family, and its restriction to a central component is the degeneration formula with the recorded assignment, retaining the joint relative evaluation.
- **claim-group-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-groups)): The separation morphism satisfies $$\label{ev:group-vfc} \varepsilon_*[\mathcal K_\Xi]^{\mathrm{vir}} =\mathop{\times}_{a=1}^u[\mathcal K_{\Xi^{(a)}}]^{\mathrm{vir}}.$$ Ordinary cotangent classes and joint evaluations of the retained positions are preserved under separation. Thus if $\Theta_a$ is a product of ordinary descendants and insertions on group $a$, and $e=(e_1,\ldots,e_u)\circ\varepsilon$, then $$\label{ev:group-correspondence} e_*\left(\prod_a\Theta_a\cap[\mathcal K_\Xi]^{\mathrm{vir}}\right) =\mathop{\times}_{a=1}^u (e_a)_*\left(\Theta_a\cap [\mathcal K_{\Xi^{(a)}}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\right).$$ The same assertion holds after grouping by connected components of a disconnected target and retaining the aligned evaluation of any chosen group. Factors with no recorded positions can therefore be preintegrated as ordinary relative invariants. The identities with algebraic insertions hold in Chow groups; for arbitrary cohomology insertions we apply the cycle class map and the cohomological projection formula.
  - Explanation: The separation morphism pushes the virtual class forward to the product of the group virtual classes, and joint evaluations and cotangent classes of retained positions are preserved.
- **claim-single-root-linearization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-linearization)): Restrict <a href="#cap:bundle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cap:bundle">[cap:bundle]</a> to classes with zero projection to $T$. For any finite bound on contact order, the connected genus-zero single-root generating series have jointly surjective linearization in the auxiliary variables. Consequently one can choose finitely many variables whose linearization is an isomorphism onto their coefficients in the contact spaces with one root.
  - Explanation: Connected genus-zero single-root series on projective bundle caps have a triangular, jointly surjective linearization onto the one-root contact spaces up to any contact bound.
- **claim-finite-cap-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-span)): For the fiber classes of <a href="#cap:bundle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cap:bundle">[cap:bundle]</a>, finitely many coefficient rows in the ordinary zero-section variables span the full dual of $\mathcal F_{\leq H}(E)$ over $\mathbb C((\hbar))$. Equivalently, after using $G_{\hbar}$ to identify states and their duals, they span all states. The empty profile and odd labels are included.
  - Explanation: Finitely many coefficient rows in ordinary zero-section variables span the full dual of the bounded contact space over Laurent series in the genus variable, including the empty profile and odd labels.
- **claim-bounded-rows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-bounded-rows)): Fix a finite list of auxiliary rows and a counting bound $d$.* 1. *For $Q$, a bound on its relative contact total bounds its effective curve degrees.* 2. *For $P$, a fixed nonnegative degree $h$ against $D_l$, together with nonnegative outgoing contact at $D_r$, bounds its effective curve degrees. Moreover its outgoing contact satisfies $a\leq h+C_0q(\beta)$, for a constant $C_0\geq0$ independent of the row.* 3. *Under these bounds every entry of the finite row matrices is a Laurent series in $\hbar$ bounded below. At each counting order there are finitely many curve classes and finitely many exponents of additional divisor fugacities.
  - Explanation: Under contact and counting bounds only finitely many curve classes occur, and each entry of the finite row matrices is a Laurent series bounded below.
- **claim-incoming-tests-Q** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-q)): Modulo $q^{d+1}$, the $Q$ cap realizes every linear functional on $\mathcal F_{\leq H}(D)$ by finitely many ordinary coefficient rows with external coefficients having nonnegative $q$-powers and Laurent $\hbar$-coefficients.
  - Explanation: Modulo a power of the counting variable the right cap realizes every linear functional on the bounded contact space by finitely many ordinary coefficient rows, by a finite inverse series.
- **claim-outgoing-states-P** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-p)): Modulo $q^{d+1}$, every prescribed state supported in $\mathcal F_{\leq H}(D)$ can be prepared by finite combinations of $P$ rows with imposed nonnegative left-section degrees. The coefficients have nonnegative $q$-powers. Every summand of the preparation, including before cancellation, has outgoing total at most $H+C_0d$ at total counting order at most $d$. The same bound holds if ordinary auxiliary insertions are replaced, removed, or assigned to a classical operation while their imposed degree conditions are retained.
  - Explanation: Every prescribed outgoing state can be prepared by the left cap with imposed section degrees, and every summand has outgoing contact within a fixed bound.
- **claim-cap-contact-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-identity)): Fix $H,d\geq0$ in either configuration. Replace a seam by a right cap $Q$ on its left and a left cap $P$ on its right. There is a finite combination of ordinary descendant coefficient rows on these caps, with external joint coefficients and the left-section coefficient selections described above, which equals the original contact gluing form on every incoming state in $\mathcal F_{\leq H}(D)$ and every outgoing state, modulo $q^{d+1}$. This is equality on the full cohomologically decorated relative state spaces, including the empty profile.* *The coefficients have nonnegative $q$-powers and bounded-below Laurent $\hbar$-expansions. At the inspected counting orders all row summands have outgoing total at most $H+C_0d$, even when their auxiliary labels undergo the modifications in <a href="#cap:P" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cap:P">24</a>. The equality holds after tensoring with any fixed external graded space and separately for each replica.
  - Explanation: Replacing a seam by a pair of caps, a finite combination of ordinary coefficient rows reproduces the original contact gluing form on all bounded incoming and all outgoing states.
- **claim-uniform-preparation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cap-uniform)): For any finite ordered list of switches and a fixed counting bound, the preceding tests can be chosen successively so that they are valid for every subset of switch replacements. All effective degrees occurring in the resulting coefficient calculations are bounded, after fixing the final nonjoining-section degree in the blowup configuration. These bounds persist when auxiliary markings are modified at any switches while their external degree selections are retained.
  - Explanation: Cap tests for a finite ordered list of switches can be chosen successively so they are valid for every subset of replacements, with effective degrees bounded throughout.
- **claim-chain-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-geometry)): For either configuration in <a href="#sew:configurations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:configurations">27</a>, chains of arbitrarily large length and all unions of capped subchains obtained from them have projective semistable realizations with smooth total space. They can be colored with two colors so that every junction has oppositely colored ends. On any fixed region disjoint from the surgeries, the component and double-stratum diagrams, boundary normal identifications, and first-page residue operations can be identified across all the realizations.
  - Explanation: Chains of any length and all capped subchains have projective semistable realizations with smooth total space and two-colorings, and local data away from surgeries agree across realizations.
- **claim-tested-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-transport)): Consider either configuration of <a href="#sew:configurations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:configurations">27</a>, with the coherent admissible singles and degree data just specified. Suppose that all three short target types satisfy the absolute Virasoro constraints in every genus, every curve class, and with all cohomology insertions. Then, for every $k>0$, every finite number $s$ of independent replicas, and every admissible test $\mathsf T$, all coefficients of $$\label{sew:tested-error} \mathsf T\left( (L_{k,X}^{(1)}Z_X^{(1)}) \otimes\bigotimes_{r=2}^{s} Z_X^{(r)} \right)$$ vanish, where $X$ is the main smooth target. The coefficients are selected by the coherent degree data, with the final exceptional degrees retained in the blowup configuration. The operator acts only on replica $1$, before the test is applied.
  - Explanation: If the three short target types satisfy the absolute Virasoro constraints, every coefficient of every admissible tensor test of the positive Virasoro error on the main smooth target vanishes.
- **claim-preparations-every-subset** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-preparation)): For any fixed finite ordered list of switches, one can choose a contact bound and cap identity test at each switch so that the following hold simultaneously for every subset of surgeries:* 1. *all incoming states lie in the chosen bounded domain;* 2. *the chosen rows themselves bound outgoing contact, even when a positive-operator summand acts on their auxiliary labels;* 3. *every coefficient calculation is finite in the degree and auxiliary-label orders under consideration and is bounded below in each genus variable;* 4. *at a switch with no exposed operation, the total capped correspondence is the original contact-gluing correspondence on all outside states.* *Each replica has its own preparation and ordinary contact gluing.
  - Explanation: Contact bounds and cap identity tests can be chosen at each switch so that incoming states are bounded, outgoing contact is bounded, all calculations are finite, and an idle switch recovers the original gluing.
- **claim-busy-switch-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-busy-bound)): There are constants $B$ and $r_0$, depending on the fixed test, $k$, and $n$, but independent of chain length, surgery subset, contact bounds and auxiliary row arities, with the following property. Each local summand in the expansion of any $\mathcal E_A$ has at most $B$ busy switches if switches and ends are separated by more than $2r_0+2$ necks. All its cohomological operations occur in recorded incidence neighborhoods of radius at most $r_0$. A switch is busy if its surgery region meets one of these neighborhoods or if an auxiliary label at that switch is used nonordinarily by $L_k$.
  - Explanation: A uniform constant bounds the number of busy switches of any local summand, independently of chain length, surgery subset, contact bounds and row arities, once switches are well separated.
- **claim-idle-switch-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-idle-equality)): Fix a local operation pattern and a switch $j$ outside its busy set. Keep its recorded component assignments and all data off the surgery region at $j$. Sum all relative-map histories, auxiliary row choices and degree decompositions inside that region. The resulting correspondence is equal with $j$ uncut and with $j$ cut and capped. This is an equality with the retained aligned evaluations and ordinary cotangent classes, before the remaining cohomological operations and tests are applied.
  - Explanation: At a switch outside the busy set, the summed correspondence is the same whether the switch is uncut or cut and capped, which allows pairwise cancellation in the alternating sum.
- **claim-primitive-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-operations)): Suppose $X$ has a detection subspace. Its primitive coevaluation and any function of either Hodge index on a primitive leg are finite linear combinations of the admissible operations of <a href="#sec:conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conventions">2</a>. The same holds for the second-index operator on the full cohomology, after decomposition into $B_0$ and $J$.
  - Explanation: With a detection subspace, the primitive coevaluation, functions of either Hodge index and the projectors are finite combinations of admissible operations.
- **claim-positive-scalar-norm** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-norm)): Let $E\in(J_\mathbb C^*)^{\otimes r}$ be a coefficient tensor obtained by applying one positive Virasoro coefficient rule, with fixed coherent $B_0$-inputs in all other slots. Its positive Hermitian squared norm can be written as a finite linear combination of admissible scalar tests of that same coefficient rule.
  - Explanation: The positive Hermitian squared norm of a primitive error tensor is a finite linear combination of admissible scalar tests of the same coefficient rule.
- **claim-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-detection)): Assume that $X$ has a detection subspace, and that all admissible scalar tests of every positive Virasoro coefficient vanish, with degree weights distinguishing the curve classes under consideration. Then $L_k^XZ_X=0$ for every $k>0$, for all cohomology insertions and in each such curve class.
  - Explanation: If a detection subspace exists and all admissible scalar tests vanish with degree weights separating curve classes, the positive Virasoro operators annihilate the potential for all insertions.
- **claim-toric-bundle-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ind-toric)): Suppose that the first-Hodge Virasoro constraints hold for $B$, in all genera and with all descendant insertions. They then hold for $E$ with the same scope. The assertion can be iterated for towers of the toric bundles described above.
  - Explanation: First-Hodge Virasoro constraints for a base pass to smooth projective toric bundles over it and to towers of such bundles.
- **claim-blowup-detection-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ind-blowup-data)): Let $M$ be an $n$-dimensional smooth complete intersection and let $S\subset M$ be a smooth codimension-two complete intersection cut by two ambient equations. Write $p:\widetilde M=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_S M\to M$, $j:E\hookrightarrow\widetilde M$, and $\pi:E\to S$. Then $$\label{ind:blowup-B0} B_0=p^*A_M\oplus j_*\pi^*A_S$$ is a nondegenerate rational Hodge subspace generated by diagonal Hodge classes. It contains all cohomology outside $H^n(\widetilde M)$ and all nonprimitive middle classes for a polarization $p^*H-\epsilon E$, with $\epsilon>0$ sufficiently small and rational. Its indicated generators have coherent single-class lifts in the blowup configuration of <a href="#sew:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:transport">29</a>.
  - Explanation: On the blowup along a codimension-two complete intersection, the span of pulled-back ambient classes and exceptional classes from the center is a nondegenerate detection subspace with coherent lifts.
- **claim-degree-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ind-degrees)): The ordinary and blowup configurations used in <a href="#ind:resolved-splitting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ind:resolved-splitting">[ind:resolved-splitting]</a> can be equipped with coherent numerical divisor tests which distinguish every curve class with a possibly nonzero ordinary Gromov–Witten contribution, after choosing very general smooth fibers when necessary. The same conclusion holds for disconnected total classes.
  - Explanation: The ordinary and blowup configurations carry coherent numerical divisor tests that separate all curve classes with possibly nonzero invariants, including disconnected total classes.
- **claim-special-fibers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ind-deformation)): Suppose the tested constraints, and hence the constraints detected by <a href="#det:detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:detection">36</a>, hold on very general members of one of the smooth complete-intersection families or nested blowup families above. They then hold on every smooth member, for every locally transported curve class.
  - Explanation: Constraints detected on very general members of the families hold on every smooth member, because the Hodge-balanced scalar tests are locally constant.

        "anchor": "stmt-fp-local-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the arities of a scalar test as in <a href=\"#sec:conventions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:conventions\">2</a>, applied to one coefficient operation of <a href=\"#conv:positive-rule\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conv:positive-rule\">2</a>. For each of its evaluation correspondences, assume the extending class and assignment-sensitive component restriction rule of <a href=\"#ev:restriction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ev:restriction\">16</a>. Assume its degree tests extend coherently and its singles are coherent admissible singles: extending algebraic classes and their complex linear combinations, or homogeneous cohomology inputs from fixed smooth projective sources through algebraic correspondences extending over the relevant total families and inducing flat Hodge maps on their smooth loci. Then its numerical value has an expansion with these properties.*\n\n1.  *Each term of its cohomological calculation uses only a bounded number of positions of the dual graph and closed incidence neighborhoods of bounded radius. The bounds depend on the fixed arities and dimensions, not on the length of inserted chains.*\n\n2.  *The terms are obtained from component restrictions of the evaluation classes, pullbacks, cups, residues, integrations, and the vertex and edge blocks of $\\Delta_{C_Y}$. A Hodge-graded coevaluation uses one vertex or one edge neighborhood.*\n\n3.  *Identical component and stratum diagrams, normal data, orders, and restriction classes in these neighborhoods give identical terms, with the same signs and Tate factors. Changing the smoothing or its connectedness away from these neighborhoods imposes no extra condition on a term.*\n\n*Ordinary singles already integrated into an evaluation class need not be counted as exposed positions. If an operator subsequently acts on one of those singles, that slot must instead be exposed and counted.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ev-configuration",
        "exact_quote": "The family $\\mathcal Q_I\\to B$ is projective and semistable, with smooth total space. Its projections to the factors of $\\mathcal Y$ are logarithmic maps. Let $I=I_0\\sqcup I_1$ be an assignment of the recorded marks to the two sides. The corresponding central component is canonically $$\\label{ev:component-product}\n  Q_{I_0,I_1}\n  =\\mathcal Q_{I_0}(X_0,D)\\times\n    \\mathcal Q_{I_1}(X_1,D).$$ When a side is disconnected, this formula can be restricted to any prescribed connected target component for each recorded mark. The aligned configuration space of all marks on a side is retained in this formula.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ev-aligned-cut",
        "exact_quote": "Fix bounded discrete data for labelled connected source components in $\\mathcal M$, permitting their disjoint union, and choose one cut of the globally aligned target expansion. Let $\\eta$ range over the resulting splittings with ordered labels on their $r$ paired relative roots of contact orders $c_1,\\ldots,c_r$. The side source graphs may contain rootless vertices or be empty. Let $\\mathcal M_{\\eta,i}$ denote the relative map stack of the *whole* side source graph over one shared side expansion. On the normalized target cut, the virtual boundary class is the sum, over these labelled splittings, of the gluing pushforwards of $$\\label{ev:aligned-cut-formula}\n \\frac{\\prod_{j=1}^r c_j}{r!}\n \\Delta_{D^r}^{!}\n \\bigl([\\mathcal M_{\\eta,0}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\n       \\times[\\mathcal M_{\\eta,1}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\\bigr).$$ There is no additional factor for a source component or a connected component of $D$. For $r=0$, the product and diagonal are the identity. The formula is an equality of virtual classes before evaluation pushforward. The $r!$ forgets the ordered root labels; the finite quotient by permutations of identical source components is taken on the whole indexed sum, not again on each root-labelled side factor.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.2"
      },

For $r=0$, the product and diagonal are the identity. The formula is an equality of virtual classes before evaluation pushforward.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ev-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "The class $\\Gamma_I$ is an actual extending correspondence on the smooth total configuration family. Let $i_\\epsilon:Q_\\epsilon\\hookrightarrow\\mathcal Q_I$ be a central component, with the recorded side and connected-component assignment $\\epsilon$. Its restriction $i_\\epsilon^*\\Gamma_I$ is given by the virtual degeneration formula restricted to splitting data with that assignment, retaining on each side the joint relative evaluation into the corresponding factor of <a href=\"#ev:component-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ev:component-product\">[ev:component-product]</a>. This is an equality of correspondence classes on $Q_\\epsilon$ before further cohomology insertions are applied.*\n\n\n*Concretely, in the scheme case let $\\eta$ range over admissible splittings with that assignment, with $r$ labelled matching relative roots of orders $c_1,\\ldots,c_r$. Let $\\mathcal M_{\\eta,0}$ and $\\mathcal M_{\\eta,1}$ be the relative moduli, and let $$G_\\eta=\\mathcal M_{\\eta,0}\\times_{D^r}\\mathcal M_{\\eta,1}.$$ The root matching is the refined diagonal pullback. With the usual labelled-root convention, the restriction is the cycle class of $$\\label{ev:restriction-formula}\n \\sum_{\\eta\\text{ of assignment }\\epsilon}\n \\frac{\\prod_{j=1}^r c_j}{r!}\\,\n (e_\\eta)_*\n \\left(\n   \\Theta_\\eta\\cap\n   \\Delta_{D^r}^{!}\n   \\bigl([\\mathcal M_{\\eta,0}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\n          \\times[\\mathcal M_{\\eta,1}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\\bigr)\n \\right).$$ Here $e_\\eta:G_\\eta\\to Q_\\epsilon$ retains the two aligned relative evaluations; it is allowed to have image on the boundary of $Q_\\epsilon$. Source-component labeling or quotient conventions are held fixed throughout. Equivalently, summing over unlabelled root data replaces $r!$ by the usual root-permutation stabilizer. For $I=\\varnothing$ the assertion is the ordinary special-fiber virtual degeneration formula.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-ev-groups",
        "exact_quote": "The separation morphism satisfies $$\\label{ev:group-vfc}\n  \\varepsilon_*[\\mathcal K_\\Xi]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\n  =\\mathop{\\times}_{a=1}^u[\\mathcal K_{\\Xi^{(a)}}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}.$$ Ordinary cotangent classes and joint evaluations of the retained positions are preserved under separation. Thus if $\\Theta_a$ is a product of ordinary descendants and insertions on group $a$, and $e=(e_1,\\ldots,e_u)\\circ\\varepsilon$, then $$\\label{ev:group-correspondence}\n e_*\\left(\\prod_a\\Theta_a\\cap[\\mathcal K_\\Xi]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\\right)\n =\\mathop{\\times}_{a=1}^u\n   (e_a)_*\\left(\\Theta_a\\cap\n           [\\mathcal K_{\\Xi^{(a)}}]^{\\mathrm{vir}}\\right).$$ The same assertion holds after grouping by connected components of a disconnected target and retaining the aligned evaluation of any chosen group. Factors with no recorded positions can therefore be preintegrated as ordinary relative invariants. The identities with algebraic insertions hold in Chow groups; for arbitrary cohomology insertions we apply the cycle class map and the cohomological projection formula.",

        "exact_quote": "Restrict <a href=\"#cap:bundle\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cap:bundle\">[cap:bundle]</a> to classes with zero projection to $T$.         "anchor": "stmt-cap-bounded-rows",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite list of auxiliary rows and a counting bound $d$.*\n\n1.  *For $Q$, a bound on its relative contact total bounds its effective curve degrees.*\n\n2.  *For $P$, a fixed nonnegative degree $h$ against $D_l$, together with nonnegative outgoing contact at $D_r$, bounds its effective curve degrees. Moreover its outgoing contact satisfies $a\\leq h+C_0q(\\beta)$, for a constant $C_0\\geq0$ independent of the row.*\n\n3.  *Under these bounds every entry of the finite row matrices is a Laurent series in $\\hbar$ bounded below.         "anchor": "stmt-cap-identity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $H,d\\geq0$ in either configuration. Replace a seam by a right cap $Q$ on its left and a left cap $P$ on its right.         "exact_quote": "Consider either configuration of <a href=\"#sew:configurations\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sew:configurations\">27</a>, with the coherent admissible singles and degree data just specified.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sew-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "For any fixed finite ordered list of switches, one can choose a contact bound and cap identity test at each switch so that the following hold simultaneously for every subset of surgeries:*\n\n1.  *all incoming states lie in the chosen bounded domain;*\n\n2.  *the chosen rows themselves bound outgoing contact, even when a positive-operator summand acts on their auxiliary labels;*\n\n3.  *every coefficient calculation is finite in the degree and auxiliary-label orders under consideration and is bounded below in each genus variable;*\n\n4.  *at a switch with no exposed operation, the total capped correspondence is the original contact-gluing correspondence on all outside states.*\n\n*Each replica has its own preparation and ordinary contact gluing.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-065.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-sew-busy-bound",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $B$ and $r_0$, depending on the fixed test, $k$, and $n$, but independent of chain length, surgery subset, contact bounds and auxiliary row arities, with the following property. Each local summand in the expansion of any $\\mathcal E_A$ has at most $B$ busy switches if switches and ends are separated by more than $2r_0+2$ necks. All its cohomological operations occur in recorded incidence neighborhoods of radius at most $r_0$. A switch is busy if its surgery region meets one of these neighborhoods or if an auxiliary label at that switch is used nonordinarily by $L_k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ind-blowup-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be an $n$-dimensional smooth complete intersection and let $S\\subset M$ be a smooth codimension-two complete intersection cut by two ambient equations. Write $p:\\widetilde M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_S M\\to M$, $j:E\\hookrightarrow\\widetilde M$, and $\\pi:E\\to S$. Then $$\\label{ind:blowup-B0}\n B_0=p^*A_M\\oplus j_*\\pi^*A_S$$ is a nondegenerate rational Hodge subspace generated by diagonal Hodge classes. It contains all cohomology outside $H^n(\\widetilde M)$ and all nonprimitive middle classes for a polarization $p^*H-\\epsilon E$, with $\\epsilon>0$ sufficiently small and rational. Its indicated generators have coherent single-class lifts in the blowup configuration of <a href=\"#sew:transport\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sew:transport\">29</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.2"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Coherent data** ([source](#stmt-conv-coherent)): Admissible singles and numerical divisor-degree tests on several degenerations are *coherent* if their restrictions agree on the common local component and stratum diagrams used in the comparison.
- **Detection subspace** ([source](#stmt-det-setup)): A *detection subspace* for a smooth projective $n$-fold $X$ is a real rational Hodge substructure $$B_0\subset H^*(X;\mathbb Q)$$ such that: 1. $B_0$ is spanned by homogeneous coherent admissible singles of type $(j,j)$; 2. the integration pairing is nondegenerate on $B_0$; 3. $B_0$ contains all cohomology outside $H^n(X)$, and contains the nonprimitive middle cohomology for some polarization. We write $J=B_0^\perp$, so $H^*(X;\mathbb C)=B_{0,\mathbb C}\oplus J_\mathbb C$ orthogonally and $J\subset H^n(X)$ is primitive.
- **Ordinary replica** ([source](#pm-h-9)): An *ordinary replica* means a separate copy of a disconnected Gromov–Witten tensor, with its own genus and degree variables.
- **Surgery** ([source](#stmt-sew-configurations)): At an internal junction, a *surgery* separates the chain and attaches a cap $Q$ to the part on the left and a cap $P$ to the part on the right.
- **Chain configuration notation** ([source](#stmt-sew-configurations)): All targets in a chain have complex dimension $n$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-positive-rule** [claim](#stmt-conv-positive-rule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-positive-rule): Expand the loop operator and quantize its quadratic Hamiltonian in blocks. -> finite list of four operations
- **s-first-page-pairing** [claim](#stmt-fp-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-pairing): Poincare duality on components and double loci plus a chain identity. -> perfect chain pairing with local inverse
- **s-first-index** [claim](#stmt-fp-first-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-first-index): Restrictions and Gysin maps preserve the first Hodge index. -> commuting index operator
- **s-ordered-comparison** [claim](#stmt-fp-ordered-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-ordered-comparison): Restrict the residue comparison to increasing tuples. -> bifiltered comparison map
- **s-pullback** [claim](#stmt-fp-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-pullback): Define the pullback on increasing tuples and check filtrations. -> bifiltered pullbacks
- **s-trace-normalization** [claim](#stmt-fp-trace-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-trace-normalization): Compare traces on a power of a relatively ample class. -> trace equals integration
- **s-leading-class** [claim](#stmt-fp-leading-class) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-leading-class): Restrict the class to components and take the top weight part. -> first-page representative
- **s-scalar-limit** [claim](#stmt-fp-scalar-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-scalar-limit): Hodge balance of derivatives of projectors, then graded contraction. -> local constancy and graded value
- **s-chain-contraction** [claim](#stmt-fp-chain-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-chain-contraction): Kunneth identification and boundaries annihilated by chain functionals. -> cohomological contraction
- **s-local-algebra** [claim](#stmt-fp-local-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-local-algebra): Pass to graded pieces, express the test through chain maps, then expand in local summands. -> bounded local expansion
- **s-configuration** [claim](#stmt-ev-configuration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-configuration): Ordered monomial charts and a cut-uniqueness argument. -> charts and central components
- **s-aligned-cut** [claim](#stmt-ev-aligned-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-aligned-cut): Virtual splitting with refined diagonal pullback and gerbe degree bookkeeping. -> labelled cut formula
- **s-restriction** [claim](#stmt-ev-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-restriction): Cartier divisor calculation then virtual splitting on each boundary divisor. -> component restriction formula
- **s-groups** [claim](#stmt-ev-groups) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-groups): Cartesian square of common expansions and direct-sum obstruction theory. -> product formula with retained evaluations
- **s-linearization** [claim](#stmt-cap-linearization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-linearization): Triangular matrix with invertible diagonal from a fiber invariant. -> surjective linearization
- **s-cap-span** [claim](#stmt-cap-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-span): Normalize by the rootless factor and apply the formal inverse function theorem. -> finite spanning rows
- **s-bounded-rows** [claim](#stmt-cap-bounded-rows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-bounded-rows): Ample degree bounds and counting of constant components. -> finiteness and Laurent bounds
- **s-cap-q** [claim](#stmt-cap-q) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-q): Finite inverse series of a perturbed invertible matrix. -> incoming tests
- **s-cap-p** [claim](#stmt-cap-p) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-p): Triangular correction downward in contact and upward in counting order. -> outgoing states with contact bound
- **s-cap-identity** [claim](#stmt-cap-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-identity): Expand the gluing form in a basis and realize each factor by caps. -> contact identity
- **s-cap-uniform** [claim](#stmt-cap-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cap-uniform): Choose bounds successively from left to right. -> bounds valid for every subset
- **s-chain-geometry** [claim](#stmt-sew-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sew-geometry): Base change and resolution of chain singularities with normal-cone degenerations. -> smooth semistable realizations
- **s-preparation** [claim](#stmt-sew-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sew-preparation): Apply the cap identity with flux estimates through each neck. -> preparations valid for every subset
- **s-busy-bound** [claim](#stmt-sew-busy-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sew-busy-bound): Count exposed positions using the positive coefficient rule and local algebra. -> uniform bound on busy switches
- **s-idle-equality** [claim](#stmt-sew-idle-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sew-idle-equality): Group the retained maps, preintegrate the cap groups and compare kernels. -> equality at an idle switch
- **s-tested-transport** [claim](#stmt-sew-transport) / [proof](#pm-h-30): Alternating sum over surgery subsets with a toggling involution at the first idle switch. -> vanishing of every tested coefficient
- **s-primitive-operations** [claim](#stmt-det-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-operations): Subtract the finite ambient coevaluation from the full coevaluation. -> primitive operations as admissible tests
- **s-positive-norm** [claim](#stmt-det-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-norm): Hodge-Riemann Hermitian form and a second replica carrying the conjugate rule. -> squared norm as scalar tests
- **s-detection** [claim](#stmt-det-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-detection): Vanishing norm forces the tensor to vanish by positivity. -> vanishing positive Virasoro operators
- **s-toric-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ind-toric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ind-toric): Hodge-difference intertwining of the loop transformation and central normalization. -> constraints transferred from base
- **s-blowup-data** [claim](#stmt-ind-blowup-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ind-blowup-data): Blowup cohomology formula and exceptional pairing computation. -> detection subspace with coherent lifts
- **s-degree-separation** [claim](#stmt-ind-degrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ind-degrees): Lefschetz and Noether-Lefschetz arguments with matching divisor pairs. -> coherent separating degree tests
- **s-deformation** [claim](#stmt-ind-deformation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ind-deformation): Local constancy of Hodge-balanced squares over the parameter space. -> constraints on every smooth member
- **s-main-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ind-deformation-2): Double induction on dimension and degree complexity through blowup and ordinary degenerations. -> constraints for every smooth complete intersection

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-virasoro-complete-intersection**: For a smooth complete intersection in projective space, the ordinary unreduced total descendant potential is annihilated by the Virasoro operators in every genus and curve class, with arbitrary complex cohomology insertions.
- **inv-primitive-detection**: If the target has a detection subspace and every admissible scalar test of each positive Virasoro coefficient vanishes, then the positive Virasoro operators annihilate the potential for all insertions in each curve class under consideration.
- **inv-tested-transport**: If the short smooth target types satisfy the absolute Virasoro constraints, then every admissible tensor test applied to the positive Virasoro error on the main smooth target vanishes, coefficient by coefficient selected by the coherent degree data.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-ungraded-primitive-insertions**: Treat primitive insertions as individually extending classes, or contractions as ungraded diagonal insertions, when transporting through a degeneration. -> Primitive classes need not extend, and the operators weight contracted indices by first Hodge degree, so naive ungraded transport gives wrong contractions.
- **anti-nonsplit-bundles**: Apply the toric-bundle transfer step used here to projectivizations of arbitrary non-split vector bundles. -> The transfer is established only for towers of projectivizations of sums of line bundles, so the argument does not cover general bundles.
- **anti-numerical-degeneration-only**: Deduce the transport statement from the numerical degeneration formula alone. -> A numerical identity does not retain the correspondence classes with recorded evaluations needed for the local Hodge calculation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence and vanishing statements proved by degeneration, cap inversion and positivity of the Hodge–Riemann form; the proof of the detection theorem argues that a norm of an error tensor is zero and hence the tensor vanishes. No finite procedure with explicit bounds is given: the constants B and r_0 and the cap rows are only shown to exist, so there is no finite constructive object to execute and test.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is an existence proof by degeneration and induction. It gives no finite procedure that a library could run, and it states only that its identities are formal coefficientwise ones. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are complex projective complete intersections and their formal descendant series. There is no state, dynamics or interaction that a simulation could model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The detection theorem reduces vanishing of the error to vanishing of scalar tests. Turning this into a checker would need explicit bases of detection subspaces and effective degeneration data, and the paper supplies neither. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sewing argument asserts only the existence of constants bounding busy switches. No explicit values or closed-form bounds are given, so no algorithm or complexity bound can be extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive coefficient rule is a finite description of operations on tensors. It is a proof device, not a representation with defined access or update costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper's own calculations are cohomological and virtual-class computations over complex varieties. They give no fixed-size arithmetic kernel or dataflow to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pairings in the paper are perfect chain pairings on cohomology complexes. They carry no hardness assumption, key or adversary model. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positivity argument shows that a Hermitian squared norm of an error tensor vanishes only if the tensor vanishes. It is an exact algebraic fact on primitive cohomology, not a statistical learning method. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only quantity that decreases is a complexity of defining degrees used to organise the induction. It is not an objective under optimisation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The switches are surgeries on a chain of geometric targets, used for an inclusion-exclusion cancellation. They are not nodes, messages or replicated state. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to pure enumerative geometry, which is mathematics rather than an engineering field. It transfers constraints between targets only for towers of toric bundles, so it gives no engineering contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-complete-intersection-scope** (hypothesis): The main result is stated only for smooth complete intersections in complex projective space, for the ordinary unreduced descendant potential. Blocked use: ['Applying the constraints to smooth projective varieties that are not complete intersections', 'Applying them to reduced potentials']
- **lim-split-bundles-only** (hypothesis): The bundle input used in the induction is toric-bundle transfer, which covers towers of projectivizations of sums of line bundles. Blocked use: ["Using this paper's bundle step for projectivizations of arbitrary non-split vector bundles"]
- **lim-primitive-insertions** (edge_case): Primitive insertions need not extend individually through a degeneration, and the Virasoro operators weight contracted indices by first Hodge degree. Blocked use: ['Transporting primitive classes through a degeneration as individually extending classes', 'Treating contractions as ungraded diagonal insertions']
- **lim-detection-subspace** (hypothesis): Detection requires a detection subspace on the target and vanishing of all admissible scalar tests, with degree weights distinguishing the curve classes considered. Blocked use: ['Concluding vanishing of the positive Virasoro operators without a detection subspace', 'Concluding per-curve-class statements when degree weights do not separate classes']
- **lim-transport-short-targets** (hypothesis): Tested transport needs the absolute Virasoro constraints on all three short target types in every genus, curve class and with all insertions, and coherent admissible singles and degree data. Blocked use: ['Invoking tested transport when the shorter targets are not known to satisfy the constraints']
- **lim-busy-bound-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The busy-switch constants are shown only to exist, depending on the test, k and the dimension; no explicit values are given. Blocked use: ['Reading off an explicit chain length or running time from the busy-switch bound', 'Treating the argument as an executable procedure']
- **lim-cap-preparation-finite-bounds** (approximation): Cap identities hold modulo a power of the counting variable and on bounded contact spaces, with finitely many rows chosen at each switch. Blocked use: ['Using the cap identity as an exact equality in all counting degrees at once', 'Using it on unbounded contact data']
- **lim-surface-curve-classes** (edge_case): Curve-class separation uses very general fibers and special handling of exceptional surfaces, then passes to every smooth member by deformation. Blocked use: ['Claiming per-curve-class results on special fibers without the deformation step']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Virasoro conjecture for the ordinary descendant Gromov–Witten theory of smooth complete intersections in projective space, in every genus and curve class and with arbitrary cohomology insertions. This includes primitive and odd cohomology classes, with no semisimplicity assumption.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Virasoro conjecture organizes the descendant Gromov–Witten invariants of a smooth projective variety into a system of differential equations. Its simplest instance is the intersection theory of the moduli spaces of curves: Witten’s conjecture and Kontsevich’s theorem identify the corresponding generating function with the distinguished solution of the KdV hierarchy (Witten 1991; Kontsevich 1992) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Witten1991](#ref-Witten1991), [Kontsevich1992](#ref-Kontsevich1992)]. Eguchi, Hori, and Xiong proposed a target-dependent Virasoro system for Fano varieties (Eguchi, Hori, and Xiong 1997) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[EHX1997](#ref-EHX1997)]. Eguchi, Jinzenji, and Xiong extended the free-field formulation to odd and off-diagonal Hodge classes, crediting Katz’s proposal to use a Hodge index in the grading (Eguchi, Jinzenji, and Xiong 1998) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[EJX1998](#ref-EJX1998)]. We use the first-Hodge-degree superspace operators and central normalization of Getzler (Getzler 1999) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Getzler1999](#ref-Getzler1999)].

For a smooth projective variety $X$, let $F_g^X(t)$ be the generating series of its ordinary, unreduced genus-$g$ descendant invariants. The variables $t_m^a$ record insertions $\tau_m(\phi_a)$ for a homogeneous cohomology basis $\{\phi_a\}$, and Novikov variables record curve classes. The total descendant potential is $$Z_X(t)=\exp\!\left(\sum_{g\geq0}\hbar^{g-1}F_g^X(t)\right).$$ The conjecture asserts $$L_k^X Z_X=0\qquad(k\geq-1),$$ where the target-dependent differential operators $L_k^X$ are formed from the Poincaré pairing, the first Hodge index $p$ on $H^{p,q}(X)$, and cup product with $c_1(TX)$. Their precise quantization, super signs, and scalar normalization are fixed in <a href="#sec:conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conventions">2</a>. All equations are formal coefficientwise identities.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X\subset\mathbb P^N_\mathbb C$ be a smooth complete intersection. Its ordinary, unreduced total descendant potential satisfies the Virasoro constraints in every genus and curve class, with arbitrary insertions from $H^*(X;\mathbb C)$.*

</div>

Thus <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the Virasoro conjecture positively for projective-space complete intersections. Primitive and odd insertions are included, with no semisimplicity hypothesis.

The genus-zero constraints are known in general. Liu and Tian proved the decisive $L_1$ and $L_2$ identities for even-class inputs without a Fano hypothesis (Liu and Tian 1998, Theorem 0.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LiuTian1998](#ref-LiuTian1998)]. Dubrovin and Zhang proved the genus-zero Frobenius-manifold formulation and the semisimple genus-one case (Dubrovin and Zhang 1999, Main Theorem) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DubrovinZhang1999](#ref-DubrovinZhang1999)]; Getzler treated the genus-zero superspace formulation used here (Getzler 1999, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Getzler1999](#ref-Getzler1999)].

Several all-genus cases are also established. Getzler proved the ordinary constraints for Calabi–Yau targets with $c_1(X)=0$ and $H^1(X)=0$ (Getzler 1999, Theorem 7.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Getzler1999](#ref-Getzler1999)]. Okounkov and Pandharipande proved the fixed-degree relative constraints for target curves, including odd descendants (Okounkov and Pandharipande 2006, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OP2003](#ref-OP2003)]. Givental’s quantized semisimple construction, together with Teleman’s classification, covers the corresponding homogeneous semisimple geometric theories (Givental 2001; Teleman 2012) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Givental2001](#ref-Givental2001), [Teleman2012](#ref-Teleman2012)]. More recently, Guo, Zhang, and Zhou proved genus-one constraints on the ambient state space of smooth Fano complete intersections and announced further full-cohomology genus-one extensions for several families, with details deferred to a forthcoming paper (Guo, Zhang, and Zhou 2026, Theorem 1 and Remark 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GuoZhangZhou2026](#ref-GuoZhangZhou2026)]. The present argument addresses all genera and all insertions simultaneously through degeneration.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Degeneration and primitive cohomology

Degeneration expresses Gromov–Witten invariants through relative invariants of simpler pieces (Li 2001, 2002; Abramovich and Fantechi 2016) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Li2001](#ref-Li2001), [Li2002](#ref-Li2002), [AF2016](#ref-AF2016)]. Relative reconstruction and equation splitting were developed by Maulik and Pandharipande (Maulik and Pandharipande 2006) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MaulikPandharipande2006](#ref-MaulikPandharipande2006)]. Argüz, Bousseau, Pandharipande, and Zvonkine used nodal invariants to reconstruct the full Gromov–Witten theory of complete intersections by induction on dimension and defining degrees (Argüz et al. 2023, Theorem 5.3 and Section 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ABPZ](#ref-ABPZ)]. We use the same geometric reduction; the additional objective is to transport the Virasoro equations, including their first-Hodge-weighted contractions.

The reduction has two stages. Factoring one defining equation and resolving the resulting family gives a degeneration $$X\rightsquigarrow U\cup_D\widetilde M,
 \qquad \widetilde M=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_S M.$$ Here $U$ and $M$ are complete intersections of smaller defining degree, while the seam $D$ and blowup center $S$ have smaller dimension. We first prove the constraints for $\widetilde M$ from those for $M$ and projective-bundle towers over $S$. We then prove them for $X$ from those for $U$, $\widetilde M$, and a ruled bundle over $D$. The needed bundles are towers of projectivizations of sums of line bundles, so toric-bundle transfer supplies their constraints from those of their bases (Coates, Givental, and Tseng 2024) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CGT](#ref-CGT)]. carries out this induction and verifies the first-Hodge convention in the bundle transfer.

Two difficulties prevent an immediate deduction from the numerical degeneration formula. Primitive insertions need not extend individually through a degeneration. Moreover, the Virasoro operators weight contracted indices by their first Hodge degrees, so their contractions cannot be treated as ungraded diagonal insertions. For fixed genus, degree, and descendant orders, we regard the coefficient of $L_k^X Z_X$ as a multilinear error tensor in its cohomology inputs. We transport scalar contractions of this tensor with coherently extending classes of fixed Hodge type, graded inverse pairings, and ordinary Gromov–Witten tensors from independent copies of the theory. These are the tests that will detect the error without extending each primitive input.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The transport argument

The transport needed in both stages rests on three constructions.

First, contractions are performed on the first page of the weight spectral sequence. Its pairing is perfect at the level of complexes and has an inverse tensor supported at vertices and edges of the degeneration graph. The first Hodge degree, including Tate twists, commutes with its differential. Multiplicative comparison and trace then evaluate the contracted tensor without choosing representatives in the surviving cohomology. We use Steenbrink’s limiting mixed Hodge structures and Fujisawa’s ordered model, multiplicative comparison, and trace (J. Steenbrink 1976; J. H. M. Steenbrink 1977; Fujisawa 2008, 2014) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Steenbrink1976](#ref-Steenbrink1976), [Steenbrink1977](#ref-Steenbrink1977), [Fujisawa2008](#ref-Fujisawa2008), [Fujisawa2014](#ref-Fujisawa2014)].

For this calculation, the virtual class pushed forward by evaluation at the recorded markings must extend to a resolved product of the family. Configurations of points on common expansions provide that product (Abramovich and Fantechi 2017) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AFConfigurations](#ref-AFConfigurations)]. We compute its component restrictions by virtual splitting before integration, keeping the recorded markings’ component assignments. When disconnected groups of maps are separated, we retain the joint evaluation of the group carrying those markings. These correspondence statements are stronger than a numerical degeneration identity and are proved in <a href="#ev:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:section">4</a>.

Second, relative caps reproduce the contact pairing at a seam. Ordinary descendants on one cap realize functionals on a bounded incoming contact space; descendants on the other prepare the corresponding states with all unwanted outgoing profiles canceled. The nonzero projective-space fiber invariants of Hu, Li, and Ruan (Hu, Li, and Ruan 2008) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HLR2008](#ref-HLR2008)] give the invertible linearization from which the full disconnected cap construction begins. In the blowup configuration, corrections of counting degree zero strictly decrease contact. Together with degree bounds, this makes every required inversion coefficientwise finite.

Insert many widely separated switches into a degeneration with a long chain of ruled components. At a switch, either keep the original joining or separate the two sides and add the prepared caps. The cap tests may use many auxiliary markings, but their ordinary insertions are integrated into the evaluation correspondence. A quadratic Virasoro operator modifies at most two existing labels. Thus the exposed cohomological operations occupy a bounded number of positions, independently of the auxiliary markings. The alternating sum over switch choices cancels by toggling the first switch away from these positions; see <a href="#fig:sewing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:sewing">1</a>. Every nonempty surgery subset smooths to a disjoint union of the shorter targets described above, whose absolute constraints are known. The cancellation therefore proves every such scalar test of the error on the original target.

Third, these tests detect all primitive tensors. Hodge–Riemann gives a positive Hermitian form on primitive middle cohomology. Pairing a Virasoro error tensor with its conjugate in a second replica produces its squared norm. The contraction is among the allowed tests, and positivity forces the tensor itself to vanish. This argument has no bound on the number of primitive insertions.

Transport initially gives coefficients selected by the divisor degrees that extend coherently through the degenerations. To obtain the asserted result in each curve class, we verify that these degrees separate all relevant classes, retaining additional divisor degrees for exceptional surfaces and blowups. Deformation invariance and the same scalar-test argument then give the constraints on every smooth member. These are part of the final induction, not extra hypotheses on <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:sewing">

<figcaption>One idle switch in the transport argument. Here $U, V$ are the ends, $C$ is a ruled neck, and $P, Q$ are replacement caps; see <a href="#sew:configurations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:configurations">27</a>. The marked neighborhoods carry the fixed cohomological operations. At a switch outside those neighborhoods, the prepared combination of cap tests reproduces the original contact identity. Toggling that switch changes the inclusion–exclusion sign and preserves the retained tensor data.</figcaption>
</figure>

\filldraw[busy] (8.2,.35) rectangle (10,1.25);
\node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=south] at (2.25,1.30) {operation positions};
\node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=south] at (9.1,1.30) {operation positions};
\foreach \i/\x/\s in {0/0/U,1/1.8/C,2/3.55/C,3/6.85/C,4/8.6/C,5/10.4/V}
  {\node[piece] (t\i) at (\x,.65) {\(\s\)};}
\draw (t0)--(t1);
\draw (t1)--(t2);
\draw (t2)--(t3);
\draw (t3)--(t4);
\draw (t4)--(t5);
\draw[densely dashed,thick] (5.2,.25)--(5.2,1.07);
\node[font=\scriptsize,above] at (5.2,1.07) {idle switch};
\draw[->,thick] (5.2,-.05)--(5.2,-.65);
\node[font=\small,right] at (5.4,-.37) {toggle};
\foreach \i/\x/\s in {0/0/U,1/1.8/C,2/3.55/C,3/6.85/C,4/8.6/C,5/10.4/V}
  {\node[piece] (b\i) at (\x,-1.25) {\(\s\)};}
\node[cappiece] (Q) at (4.6,-1.25) {\(Q\)};
\node[cappiece] (P) at (5.8,-1.25) {\(P\)};
\draw (b0)--(b1);

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Organization and scope of the inputs

specifies the operators, tensor tests, and completions. [fp:section](#fp:section), [ev:section](#ev:section) establish the local Hodge calculation and its evaluation correspondence. [cap:section](#cap:section) constructs the cap tests, and <a href="#sew:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:section">6</a> proves their transport theorem. [sec:detection](#sec:detection) gives the positivity detector. [ind:section](#ind:section) assembles these results in the complete-intersection induction, including the surface and curve-class arguments.

Established degeneration, limiting-Hodge, toric-bundle, and intersection-theoretic results are used with their stated hypotheses. The local correspondence calculation, bounded cap preparation, grouped switch cancellation, and primitive tensor detector are proved here. In particular, the sewing theorem is not attributed to the numerical degeneration formula. The toric-bundle step explicitly passes from a half-total-degree convention to first Hodge degree at Hodge-neutral auxiliary parameters, with the corresponding central normalization.

<a id="sec:conventions"></a>
### Operators, tensor tests, and conventions

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The descendant theory

All varieties are smooth and projective over $\mathbb C$, unless they occur as fibers of a specified semistable family. Cohomology has complex coefficients and its usual parity. Tensor products, symmetric powers, permutations, and derivatives are taken in super vector spaces. For a target $X$ of complex dimension $n$, write $$(a,b)_X=\int_X a\cup b,\qquad
 \mu_X|_{H^{p,q}(X)}=(p-n/2)\mathrm{id},\qquad
 \mathcal R_X(a)=c_1(TX)\cup a.$$ The pairing is perfect and even. Its coevaluation is the categorical inverse of this pairing; it includes the signs dictated by the super symmetry. We never insert a second parity involution into a node kernel.

<a id="eq:potential"></a>
<a id="eq:partition"></a>
Choose a Hodge-homogeneous basis $\{\phi_a\}$ and let $t(z)=\sum_{m\geq0,a}t_m^a\phi_a z^m$, where $t_m^a$ has the parity of $\phi_a$. The connected potentials and total descendant potential are $$\begin{aligned}
 F_g^X(t)
 &=\sum_{\beta}\sum_{r\geq0}
   \frac{\mathsf Q^\beta}{r!}
   \int_{[\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,r}(X,\beta)]^\mathrm{vir}}
        \prod_{i=1}^r\left(\sum_{m,a}t_m^a\psi_i^m\mathop{\mathrm{ev}}_i^*\phi_a\right),
 \label{eq:potential}\\
 Z_X(t)&=\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq0}\hbar^{g-1}F_g^X(t)\right).
 \label{eq:partition}
\end{aligned}$$ Only stable maps contribute. The $\psi_i$ are cotangent classes at markings on the source of the stable map, not classes pulled back by stabilization to curves. In particular, the same convention is used in relative theories and in the fiber calculation below.

Equation <a href="#eq:partition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:partition">[eq:partition]</a> packages disconnected domains, with the empty domain contributing $1$. We use labelled coefficient extraction to discuss multilinear tensors: distinct formal variables distinguish all prescribed insertions, even when their values coincide. This convention accounts automatically for the factorials in <a href="#eq:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:potential">[eq:potential]</a>.

All statements are coefficientwise. Fixing an ample integral curve degree, a finite list of markings, and a genus coefficient leaves finite sums of effective curve classes and discrete stable-map data. In intermediate relative theories we impose the additional contact and end-degree bounds specified in the cap construction. Laurent series in $\hbar$ are always bounded below at the finite auxiliary-variable and degree orders in use. For several replicas, the genus and curve variables are independent. The cap construction proves the corresponding boundedness when the counting polarization is only relatively ample.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The positive Virasoro operators

Let $$\mathcal H_X=H^*(X)((z^{-1})),\qquad
 \Omega_X(f,g)=\operatorname*{Res}_{z=0}(f(-z),g(z))_X\,dz.$$ With the standard polarization $\mathcal H_X=H^*(X)[z]\oplus z^{-1}H^*(X)[[z^{-1}]]$, set <a id="eq:loop-operators"></a>$$\ell_0=z\partial_z+\tfrac12+\mu_X+\frac{\mathcal R_X}{z},
 \qquad
 \ell_k=\ell_0(z\ell_0)^k\quad(k\geq1).
 \label{eq:loop-operators}$$ These are infinitesimal symplectic operators. For example, $\mu_X^*=-\mu_X$, $\mathcal R_X^*=\mathcal R_X$, and the sign from $z\mapsto-z$ gives the required adjoints. The quadratic Hamiltonian of $\ell_k$ is quantized with normal ordering. In Darboux coordinates this sends terms $pp,pq,qq$, respectively, to $\hbar$ times second derivatives, first-order vector fields, and multiplication by a quadratic polynomial divided by $\hbar$. Apply the dilaton translation $$q(z)=t(z)-z\,1_X.$$ Up to the harmless simultaneous operator-sign convention, these are the positive operators $L_k^X$ in Getzler’s formulation (Getzler 1999, Equation (1.2)) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Getzler1999](#ref-Getzler1999)]. There is no central scalar correction for $k>0$. We fix the signs by that reference when invoking the Virasoro commutators.

For clarity, the proof uses the following finite description rather than a coordinate expansion of every coefficient.

<a id="stmt-conv-positive-rule"></a>
<div id="conv:positive-rule" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Positive coefficient rule). *A labelled coefficient of $L_k^XZ_X$, for fixed $k>0$, is a finite linear combination of the following operations on the disconnected Gromov–Witten tensors:*

1.  *add one special unit marking, with prescribed descendant and $\mathcal R_X$-powers;*

2.  *act on one existing marking, changing its descendant exponent and applying an $\mathcal R_X$-power, with a coefficient polynomial in its first Hodge degree;*

3.  *add two markings joined by the coevaluation of $(\, ,\,)_X$, with descendant powers, first-Hodge-degree polynomials, and $\mathcal R_X$-powers on its legs;*

4.  *remove two primary markings, leaving their two cohomology inputs in the classical pairing with $\mathcal R_X^{k+1}$, multiplied by the remaining disconnected Gromov–Witten tensor.*

*The numerical coefficients and genus powers are universal for targets of fixed dimension. At most two existing labels are used nonordinarily in one summand.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-conv-positive-rule"><summary>Proof of stmt-conv-positive-rule</summary>

*Proof.* Expand $\ell_0(z\ell_0)^k$ using $[z\partial_z,z]=z$ and $[\mu_X,\mathcal R_X]=\mathcal R_X$. The resulting coefficients are polynomials in the first Hodge index, composed with Chern-class powers. The three blocks of its quadratic Hamiltonian give the last three types of operation under normal ordering; the dilaton shift gives the special unit marking. The block with both variables on the positive polarization has its only relevant negative shift at the primary pair, giving the classical $\mathcal R_X^{k+1}$-pairing. This is also the three-block decomposition in the cited coordinate formula. Since the Hamiltonian is quadratic, at most two existing labels are involved. Super quantization uses the same calculation with graded derivatives and permutations. ◻

</details>

The two lower operators are the standard string operator $L_{-1}$ and the quantization of $\ell_0$ with scalar $$C_X=\frac{\chi(X)}{16}-\frac14\mathop{\mathrm{str}}(\mu_X^2),$$ in Getzler’s first-Hodge convention; its Chern-number form appears in <a href="#ind:central-normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ind:central-normalization">[ind:central-normalization]</a>. The nonpositive constraints require no new transport theorem. The string equation gives $L_{-1}Z_X=0$. Together with $L_1Z_X=0$, the commutator $[L_1,L_{-1}]=2L_0$ gives $L_0Z_X=0$ with this normalization. Equivalently one may use the usual dilaton, homogeneity, divisor, and genus-one Riemann–Roch identities. We therefore prove all positive constraints.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Admissible scalar tests

An *ordinary replica* means a separate copy of a disconnected Gromov–Witten tensor, with its own genus and degree variables. A *tested positive constraint* consists of one coefficient rule from <a href="#conv:positive-rule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:positive-rule">2</a>, applied to one specified replica, followed by a finite tensor contraction using:

1.  ordinary replicas and classical cup integrals;

2.  single classes obtained from algebraic classes on the relevant total families, allowing complex linear combinations and including units and Chern-class powers, or from homogeneous cohomology classes of fixed smooth projective sources through algebraic correspondences extending over those total families and inducing flat Hodge maps on their smooth loci;

3.  coevaluations joining arbitrary pairs of slots, possibly on different replicas;

4.  polynomials, or functions on the finite spectrum, of the first Hodge degree on any exposed leg;

5.  cups with admissible single classes and numerical coefficients.

The arities of this test are fixed. Only the chosen replica is acted on by $L_k$; the test is then applied linearly to its coefficients. The test may be different for each coefficient that is to be proved zero.
